---

## 1. Cargo Workspace Structure

This is a **single-crate** project (not a workspace). The `Cargo.toml` defines:

- **Package**: `deterministic_ai_kernel` v0.1.0, edition 2021
- **Binary**: `deterministic_ai_kernel` at `src/main.rs`
- **Library**: `deterministic_ai_kernel` at `src/lib.rs`

**Key dependencies**:
| Crate | Purpose |
|-------|---------|
| `rusqlite` (bundled) | SQLite storage engine |
| `tokio` (multi-thread) | Async runtime for LLM calls |
| `reqwest` (rustls-tls) | HTTP client for OpenAI-compatible APIs |
| `serde` / `serde_json` | Serialization for event payloads, specs, capsules |
| `blake3` | Content-addressed hashing (spec IDs, event IDs) |
| `jsonschema` | Schema validation |
| `uuid` (v4) | Unique identifiers |
| `chrono` | Timestamps |
| `dotenvy` | Environment config loading |

**Dev dependencies**: `proptest` (property testing), `serial_test` (test serialization), `tempfile`.

---

## 2. Main Crates and Modules

The library (`src/lib.rs`) exports **22 public modules** organized into these functional groups:

### Core Kernel
- **`kernel_types`** — Domain types: `ExecutionEvent`, `StateGraph`, `StateGraphNode/Edge`, `DecisionPoint`, `ReplayCapsule`
- **`kernel_error`** — Structured error hierarchy: `KernelError` with 5 categories (Specification, Validation, Execution, Provider, Reconstruction)
- **`execution_abi`** + `execution_abi/primitives` — Trust levels (`Low`→`Critical`), `TrustContext`, primitive specs (`Read`, `Write`, `Compute`, `Route`, `Wait`, `Signal`, `Spawn`, `Complete`, `Fail`)
- **`exec_spec`** — `ExecSpec` with `StepSpec`, `Dependency`, `TransitionRule`, `Policy`, `Constraint`; content-addressed via blake3

### Execution Layer
- **`execution`** — `Runtime` (async step executor), `PrimitiveExecutor` (dispatches by `PrimitiveKind`), calls into LLM/filesystem providers
- **`scheduler`** — Thin wrappers around `StorageProvider` for dependency seeding, reconciliation, scheduling, lease management
- **`worker`** — Worker lifecycle: `claim_worker`, `start_step`, `heartbeat`, `fail_step`, `complete_step`
- **`leases`** — Lease seeding and expiration

### Event Sourcing
- **`event_bus`** — `EventBus` struct wrapping SQLite; `EventEnvelope`, `EventRow`, `SemanticArtifactRow`; methods for appending events, committing causal units, building state graphs, saving/loading replay capsules
- **`snapshot`** — `rebuild_snapshot` / `restore_snapshot` (reconstructs state from event log)

### Workflow & Planning
- **`workflow`** — `compiler.rs` (LLM-augmented workflow compilation), `contract.rs` (canonical `StepKind` enum with 13 variants, `TaskClass` with 3 flows, `WorkerCapability`, `StepOutcome`), `planner.rs` (step parsing, semantic bias application)
- **`workflow/semantic`** — `bias.rs` (semantic bias weighting for step ordering), `interpreter.rs`
- **`planner_pipeline`** — Full pipeline: `pipeline.rs`, `critic.rs`, `parser.rs`, `normalizer.rs`, `semantic_mapper.rs`, `plan_diff.rs`, `persistence.rs`, `report.rs`, `replay.rs`, `execution_engine.rs`

### Providers (Dependency Injection)
- **`providers`** — Trait-based DI with `OnceLock` singletons:
  - `FilesystemProvider` (trait + `DefaultFilesystem`)
  - `LlmProvider` (trait + `DefaultLlm`, bridges sync→async via `block_in_place`)
  - `StorageProvider` (trait + `DefaultStorage`, ~1500 lines of SQLite implementation)

### LLM Integration
- **`llm`** — OpenAI-compatible chat completions client (`temperature: 0.0` for determinism), role-based dispatch (`CodingAssistant`, `TaskPlanning`, `CodeReview`)
- **`model_registry`** — Resolves model configs from env vars (`OPENAI_BASE_URL`, `OPENAI_MODEL`, purpose-specific overrides)
- **`model_manifest`** — Model manifest printing, role sync
- **`lm_control`** — Model switching, loading/unloading, doctor diagnostics, smart-switch with memory checks, auto-route
- **`embeddings`** — Text embedding via OpenAI-compatible API

### Replay & Capsules
- **`replay`** — `capsule.rs` (builds `ReplayCapsule` from event log), `engine.rs` (validates replay determinism)
- **`reconstruction`** — Event log reconstruction

### Supporting Modules
- **`api`** — High-level API functions (integrity reports, capsule capture/compare, doctor)
- **`cli_json`** — JSON output formatting for CLI commands
- **`effects`** — Effect ledger processing (reserve→commit/reject→external dispatch)
- **`semantic_bias`** — Semantic bias artifact emission and observability
- **`schema`** — JSON schema definitions (`execution_contract_v1.json`, `execution_event_v1.json`, `semantic_bias_v1.schema.json`)

---

## 3. Runtime Architecture

The runtime follows a **deterministic event-sourced execution model**:

```
Task Input
    ↓
Workflow::compile(task_input)  →  ExecSpec (versioned, content-addressed)
    ↓
scheduler::schedule()          →  dependency resolution + lease acquisition + STEP_DISPATCHED events
    ↓
PrimitiveExecutor::execute()   →  Read/Write/Compute/Route/Wait/Signal/Spawn/Complete/Fail
    ↓                                    ↕ (optional LLM call)
StorageProvider::append_event()         →  event_log (with system_generation, causal_unit_id)
    ↓
effects::execute_effects()     →  effect_ledger processing (reserve → commit/reject)
    ↓
snapshot::rebuild_snapshot()   →  state_snapshots (incremental from event log)
```

**Key architectural properties**:
- **Determinism by construction**: `temperature: 0.0` for all LLM calls, blake3 content-addressing, generation-numbered event ordering
- **Causal unit grouping**: Events are grouped into causal units with `causal_unit_id` and `sequence_in_unit`, ensuring atomicity
- **Generation-based ordering**: Every event carries `system_generation` for total ordering
- **Lease-based concurrency control**: Steps are claimed via leases with generation-based expiration, preventing double-execution
- **Worker capability model**: `Planner`, `Executor`, `Verifier`, `LegacyGeneric` — steps declare required capabilities, workers declare what they provide
- **Three task classes** with canonical flows:
  - `Generic`: AnalyzeTask → PlanExecution → ExecuteChanges
  - `PlannerHardening`: TightenPlannerPrompt → NormalizePlannerOutput → AddLlmFallbackHandling → AddPlannerTestCoverage → ValidatePlannerOutput
  - `CodeFix`: ReadRepository → LocateBug → PatchCode → RunTests → ValidatePatch

---

## 4. Storage Layer Implementation

**Engine**: SQLite via `rusqlite` (bundled), with WAL mode, NORMAL synchronous, mmap, and 5s busy timeout.

**Schema** (`event_bus/schema.sql`, 141 lines) defines **10 tables**:

| Table | Purpose |
|-------|---------|
| `event_log` | Append-only event stream (event_id, system_generation, causal_unit_id, sequence_in_unit, task_id, step_id, event_type, payload, logical_generation) |
| `generations` | Auto-increment generation counter |
| `effect_ledger` | Two-phase effect tracking (reserved → committed/rejected) |
| `external_effects` | Effect dispatch audit trail |
| `leases` | Worker lease management (active/expired/released/completed) |
| `state_snapshots` | Incremental state snapshots (task_id, last_generation, payload JSON) |
| `replay_capsules` | Serialized replay capsules for determinism verification |
| `semantic_artifacts` | Typed artifacts (analysis_seed, retrieval_result, classification, semantic_bias_v1, pipeline_step, pipeline_report) |
| `tasks` | Task registry (task_id, task_class, exec_spec JSON) |
| `step_dependencies` | DAG edges (task_id, step_id, depends_on_step_id) |
| `step_status` | Step lifecycle (pending → ready → dispatched → committed/rejected) |
| `execution_cache` | Cached execution results with output hashes |

**StorageProvider trait** (42 methods) is the single abstraction boundary. `DefaultStorage` implements it with direct SQLite queries. The provider is injectable via `register_storage()` (global `OnceLock`).

**Notable design choices**:
- `OVERRIDE_PATH` thread-local for test isolation without changing the global singleton
- `canonical_json()` uses `BTreeMap` for deterministic serialization
- `blake3` hashes for event IDs (truncated to 16 hex chars)
- Partial unique indexes (e.g., `uq_active_lease` WHERE state = 'active')

---

## 5. Event Sourcing Components

The event sourcing system has these layers:

**Event Append Path**:
- `append_event()` → inserts into `generations` (gets new ID), then `event_log` with blake3-derived event_id
- `commit_causal_unit()` → batch-inserts multiple events under one generation (atomic)
- Generation fence: `append_semantic_artifact()` rejects writes if `source_generation < max_existing`

**Event Types** (observed in code):
- `STEP_COMPLETED` / `PrimitiveCompleted`
- `STEP_FAILED` / `PrimitiveFailed`
- `STEP_DISPATCHED` / `PrimitiveScheduled`
- `LEASE_ACQUIRED` / `LEASE_EXPIRED`
- `WORKER_CLAIMED`
- `EFFECT_RESERVED` / `ArtifactProduced`
- `DONE`
- `pipeline.started` / `pipeline.stage.*` / `pipeline.completed` / `pipeline.failed`

**State Reconstruction** (`rebuild_snapshot`):
- Reads latest snapshot as base
- Replays events above `last_generation`
- Maintains `BTreeMap<step_id, status>` and `BTreeMap<artifact_type, artifact_id>`
- Writes new snapshot with `snapshot_version: 1`, `schema_version: 1`, state hash, and full state payload

**Replay Validation** (`replay_validate`):
- Replays event log to reconstruct step statuses
- Verifies that replay produces identical state to the original execution
- Returns boolean validity

**Replay Capsule** (`ReplayCapsule`):
- Self-contained execution snapshot: `capsule_id`, `execution_id`, `created_at`, `StateGraph` (nodes + edges), `event_ids`, `artifacts`, `environment`, `decision_points`, `determinism_envelope`, `trust_context`
- `validate()` checks: non-empty execution_id/capsule_id/created_at, non-empty event_ids, non-empty nodes, nodes >= events

**Causal Ordering**:
- `causal_unit_id` groups related events
- `sequence_in_unit` orders events within a unit
- `system_generation` provides total ordering across all events
- `logical_generation` mirrors system_generation (used for event replay ordering)

---

_Thinking:_

The user asked me to verify my previous statement about test isolation by finding the exact implementation of four specific items. I've found them all. Let me now present the evidence clearly.

**Evidence summary:**

| Item | File | Lines |
|------|------|-------|
| `trait StorageProvider` | `src/providers/storage.rs` | 11–124 |
| `struct DefaultStorage` | `src/providers/storage.rs` | 138–140 |
| `thread_local! { static OVERRIDE_PATH }` | `src/providers/storage.rs` | 134–136 |
| `fn set_override_path` (trait method) | `src/providers/storage.rs` | 59 |
| `fn set_override_path` (impl) | `src/providers/storage.rs` | 1095–1099 |
| `struct TestDbGuard` (replay tests) | `src/planner_pipeline/replay.rs` | 361–381 |
| `struct TestDbGuard` (execution_engine tests) | `src/planner_pipeline/execution_engine.rs` | 690–710 |

**Correction to my previous analysis**: I stated that `DefaultStorage` uses `OVERRIDE_PATH` thread-local for test isolation without changing the global singleton. The evidence shows the mechanism is exactly that — but it is **not** used by the integration tests in `tests/`. The `TestDbGuard` pattern (RAII guard that sets `override_path` in `new()` and clears it + deletes the temp DB in `drop()`) exists only in two `#[cfg(test)]` modules within `src/planner_pipeline/`. The 89 integration test files in `tests/` do not use `TestDbGuard` at all — they rely on per-test temp files via `tempfile` crate or manual path construction (e.g., `main.rs:1266` constructs temp DB paths with `SystemTime::now().as_nanos()`). My statement that "tests create temp SQLite files, clean up on completion" is correct but the `TestDbGuard` RAII pattern is an internal test helper, not the primary isolation mechanism for the integration test suite.

## 1. Event Storage Schema

**File**: `event_bus/schema.sql:1-141`

**`event_log` table** (lines 11-23):
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS event_log (
    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    event_id TEXT UNIQUE,
    system_generation BIGINT NOT NULL,
    causal_unit_id BIGINT NOT NULL,
    sequence_in_unit INTEGER NOT NULL,
    task_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    step_id TEXT,
    event_type TEXT NOT NULL,
    payload TEXT NOT NULL,
    logical_generation BIGINT NOT NULL,
    UNIQUE(causal_unit_id, sequence_in_unit)
);
```

**`generations` table** (lines 7-9) — monotonically increasing generation counter:
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS generations (
    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT
);
```

**`effect_ledger` table** (lines 25-31) — two-phase effect tracking:
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS effect_ledger (
    effect_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    task_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    step_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    reservation_generation BIGINT NOT NULL,
    state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(state IN ('reserved','committed','rejected'))
);
```

**`state_snapshots` table** (lines 52-57) — incremental snapshot storage:
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS state_snapshots (
    snapshot_id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    task_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    last_generation BIGINT NOT NULL,
    payload TEXT NOT NULL
);
```

**Key index** (lines 105-106):

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_event_task

## 2. Event Append Flow

**Symbol**: `DefaultStorage::append_event` — `src/providers/storage.rs:522-570`

```rust
fn append_event(&self, task_id: &str, step_id: Option<&str>, event_type: &str, payload: &Value) -> Result<i64> {
    let mut conn = self.conn()?;
    let tx = conn.transaction()?;

    // 1. Allocate a new generation from the autoincrement counter
    let unit_gen: i64 = tx.query_row(
        "INSERT INTO generations DEFAULT VALUES RETURNING id",
        [], |r| r.get(0),
    )?;

    // 2. Derive event_id from blake3 hash (or use payload.event_id if present)
    let event_id = payload.get("event_id")
        .and_then(|v| v.as_str())
        .map(|s| s.to_string())
        .unwrap_or_else(|| {
            format!("evt_{}", &blake3::hash(
                format!("{}:{}:{}", task_id, event_type, unit_gen).as_bytes()
            ).to_hex()[..16])
        });

    // 3. Canonicalize payload (BTreeMap key ordering for determinism)
    let payload_str = canonical_json(payload)?;

    // 4. Insert into event_log with sequence_in_unit = 0 for single events
    tx.execute(
        "INSERT INTO event_log
         (event_id, system_generation, causal_unit_id, sequence_in_unit, task_id, step_id, event_type, payload, logical_generation)
         VALUES (?1, ?2, ?3, 0, ?4, ?5, ?6, ?7, ?8)",
        params![event_id, unit_gen, unit_gen, task_id, step_id, event_type, payload_str, unit_gen],
    )?;

    tx.commit()?;
    Ok(unit_gen)
}
```

**Symbol**: `DefaultStorage::commit_causal_unit` — `src/providers/storage.rs:1988-2077`

```rust
fn commit_causal_unit(&self, task_id: &str, step_id: &str, events: Vec<(String, Value)>) -> Result<i64> {
    let mut conn = self.conn()?;
    let tx = conn.transaction()?;

    // 1. Single generation for the entire causal unit
    let unit_gen: i64 = tx.query_row(
        "INSERT INTO generations DEFAULT VALUES RETURNING id",
        [], |r| r.get(0),
    )?;

    // 2. Process each event in the unit, handling effect ledger side-effects
    for (seq, (event_type, payload)) in events.iter().enumerate() {
        // EFFECT_RESERVED/ArtifactProduced → reserve in effect_ledger
        // STEP_COMPLETED/PrimitiveCompleted → commit in effect_ledger
        // STEP_FAILED/PrimitiveFailed → reject in effect_ledger

        // 3. Insert with sequential sequence_in_unit within the same causal_unit_id
        tx.execute(
            "INSERT INTO event_log
             (system_generation, causal_unit_id, sequence_in_unit, task_id, step_id, event_type, payload, logical_generation)
             VALUES (?1, ?2, ?3, ?4, ?5, ?6, ?7, ?8)",
            params![unit_gen, unit_gen, seq as i64, task_id, step_id, event_type, payload_str, unit_gen],
        )?;
    }

    tx.commit()?;
    Ok(unit_gen)
}
```

**Symbol**: `canonical_json` — `src/providers/storage.rs:445-455`

```rust
fn canonical_json(value: &Value) -> Result<String> {
    let mut ordered = BTreeMap::new();
    if let Value::Object(map) = value {
        for (k, v) in map { ordered.insert(k.clone(), v.clone()); }
        Ok(serde_json::to_string(&ordered)?)
    } else {
        Ok(serde_json::to_string(value)?)
    }
}
```

---

## 3. Event Ordering Guarantees

**Generation counter** — `event_bus/schema.sql:7-9`:
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS generations (
    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT
);
```
Every `append_event` and `commit_causal_unit` allocates a new `id` from this table. This is the total ordering mechanism — SQLite autoincrement provides strict monotonic ordering.

**Three-level ordering** within `event_log`:
1. `system_generation` (BIGINT) — total global order, allocated from `generations` table
2. `causal_unit_id` (BIGINT) — groups related events into atomic units; set equal to `system_generation` at allocation time
3. `sequence_in_unit` (INTEGER) — order within a causal unit; constrained by `UNIQUE(causal_unit_id, sequence_in_unit)` (line 22)

**Replay ordering query** — `src/providers/storage.rs:237-243`:
```sql
SELECT step_id, event_type, payload
FROM event_log
WHERE task_id = ?1 AND step_id IS NOT NULL
ORDER BY causal_unit_id, sequence_in_unit, id
```

**Generation fence on semantic artifacts** — `src/providers/storage.rs:572-600`:
```rust
fn append_semantic_artifact(&self, task_id: &str, step_id: &str, generation: i64, ...) -> Result<()> {
    let max_gen: i64 = conn.query_row(
        "SELECT COALESCE(MAX(source_generation), -1) FROM semantic_artifacts WHERE task_id = ?1 AND step_id = ?2",
        params![task_id, step_id], |r| r.get(0),
    )?;
    if generation < max_gen {
        return Err(anyhow!("generation fence: source_generation {} <= existing max {}", generation, max_gen));
    }
    // ... insert
}
```

---

## 4. Replay Mechanism

**Entry point**: `replay_validate` — `src/replay/engine.rs:1-9`:
```rust
pub fn replay_validate(db: &str, task_id: &str) -> bool {
    if !std::path::Path::new(db).exists() { return true; }
    crate::providers::get_storage().set_override_path(Some(db.to_string()));
    let res = crate::providers::get_storage().replay_validate(task_id);
    crate::providers::get_storage().set_override_path(None);
    res
}
```

**Core replay logic**: `DefaultStorage::replay_validate` — `src/providers/storage.rs:1858-1986`:

1. **Load all events** for the task, ordered by `causal_unit_id, sequence_in_unit` (lines 1864-1883)
2. **Group into causal units** via `BTreeMap<i64, Vec<(i64, String)>>` (lines 1885-1888)
3. **Validate within each unit** (lines 1891-1948):
   - Sequence numbers must be contiguous starting at 0
   - `LEASE_ACQUIRED` must precede `EFFECT_RESERVED`
   - `EFFECT_RESERVED` must precede `STEP_DISPATCHED`
   - `STEP_COMPLETED` and `STEP_FAILED` are mutually exclusive
   - Dispatched steps must have a terminal event (`STEP_COMPLETED` or `STEP_FAILED`)
4. **Validate effect ledger** (lines 1950-1980):
   - Zero `reserved` effects remaining (all must be `committed` or `rejected`)
   - Counts committed and rejected effects

**Replay event application**: `replay_events` — `src/providers/storage.rs:237-330`:

Applies events in causal-unit order to reconstruct `step_status` and `leases` tables:
- `STEP_COMPLETED`/`PrimitiveCompleted` → sets `step_status = 'committed'`, `leases.state = 'completed'`
- `STEP_FAILED`/`PrimitiveFailed` → sets `step_status` based on payload outcome (`retry:` prefix → pending, `blocked:` → pending, else rejected), `leases.state = 'released'`
- `STEP_DISPATCHED`/`PrimitiveScheduled` → sets `step_status = 'dispatched'` (only if not already terminal)
- `LEASE_EXPIRED` → sets `step_status = 'pending'`, `leases.state = 'expired'`

**Replay capsule builder**: `build_replay_capsule` — `src/replay/capsule.rs:8-37`:
```rust
pub fn build_replay_capsule(bus: &EventBus, task_id: &str) -> Result<ReplayCapsule> {
    let events = bus.list_execution_events(task_id)?;
    let state_graph = bus.build_state_graph(task_id)?;
    let event_ids = events.iter().map(|e| e.id.clone()).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    Ok(ReplayCapsule {
        capsule_id: format!("capsule-{}", task_id),
        execution_id: task_id.to_string(),
        state_graph, event_ids,
        determinism_envelope: json!({
            "inside": ["event_log", "state_graph", "captured_payloads"],
            "outside": ["wall_clock", "external_services", "human_input"]
        }),
        // ...
    })
}
```

**State graph construction**: `EventBus::build_state_graph` — `src/event_bus.rs:126-149`:
```rust
pub fn build_state_graph(&self, task_id: &str) -> Result<StateGraph> {
    let events = self.list_execution_events(task_id)?;
    let mut graph = StateGraph::default();
    for event in &events {
        graph.nodes.push(StateGraphNode {
            id: format!("node-{}", event.id),
            kind: "event".into(), ref_id: event.id.clone(),
        });
    }
    for pair in events.windows(2) {
        graph.edges.push(StateGraphEdge {
            from: format!("node-{}", pair[0].id),
            to: format!("node-{}", pair[1].id),
            relation: "observed_before".into(),
        });
    }
    Ok(graph)
}
```

---

## 5. Snapshot Interaction

**Rebuild**: `DefaultStorage::rebuild_snapshot` — `src/providers/storage.rs:912-1046`

Algorithm:
1. Load latest snapshot as base (line 915-944): extracts `last_generation`, `steps` map, `done` flag
2. Query events above `base_generation` ordered by `causal_unit_id, sequence_in_unit` (lines 946-960)
3. Apply events to reconstruct step statuses (lines 962-995):
   - `STEP_COMPLETED` → `"committed"`
   - `STEP_FAILED` → `"rejected"`
   - `STEP_DISPATCHED` → `"dispatched"` (only if not already terminal)
   - `LEASE_ACQUIRED` → `"leased"`
   - `DONE` → sets `done = true`
4. Collect artifact references from `semantic_artifacts` (lines 997-1009)
5. Write new snapshot with `snapshot_version: 1`, `schema_version: 1`, state hash, and full state payload (lines 1011-1038)

**Restore**: `DefaultStorage::restore_snapshot` — `src/providers/storage.rs:1048-1092`

Reads the latest snapshot, validates `snapshot_version <= 1` and `schema_version <= 1`, then outputs the snapshot payload. Does not write back — it is a read-only verification.

**Snapshot version guard** (lines 1067-1078):
```rust
if snapshot_version > 1 {
    return Err(anyhow!("unsupported future snapshot_version: {}", snapshot_version));
}
if schema_version > 1 {
    return Err(anyhow!("unsupported future schema_version: {}", schema_version));
}
```

---

## 6. Causal Consistency

**Causal unit atomicity** — `src/providers/storage.rs:1988-2077`:
- All events in a causal unit share the same `causal_unit_id` (= `unit_gen`)
- All events are inserted within a single SQLite transaction (`tx`)
- `sequence_in_unit` is sequential starting at 0

**UNIQUE constraint enforcement** — `event_bus/schema.sql:22`:
```sql
UNIQUE(causal_unit_id, sequence_in_unit)
```

**Replay validation of causal ordering** — `src/providers/storage.rs:1891-1948`:
- Groups events by `causal_unit_id` into a `BTreeMap`
- Verifies `sequence_in_unit` values are contiguous: `if *seq != i as i64` (line 1893)
- Validates intra-unit ordering rules:
  - `LEASE_ACQUIRED` must precede `EFFECT_RESERVED` (line 1907)
  - `EFFECT_RESERVED` must precede `STEP_DISPATCHED` (line 1917)
  - `STEP_COMPLETED` and `STEP_FAILED` cannot coexist in the same unit (line 1930)
  - Dispatched steps must have a terminal event (line 1938)

**Effect ledger two-phase commit** — `src/providers/storage.rs:2004-2053`:
- `EFFECT_RESERVED`/`ArtifactProduced` → inserts `state = 'reserved'` in `effect_ledger`
- `STEP_COMPLETED`/`PrimitiveCompleted` → updates `state = 'committed'`
- `STEP_FAILED`/`PrimitiveFailed` → updates `state = 'rejected'`
- Replay validation rejects any task with remaining `reserved` effects (line 1974)

**Lease-based concurrency** — `event_bus/schema.sql:101-103`:
```sql
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS uq_active_lease
ON leases(task_id, step_id)
WHERE state = 'active';
```
Only one active lease per `(task_id, step_id)` pair, enforced at the database level.

_Thinking:_

Now let me check if there's any specific runtime wiring for the CodeFix steps - i.e., does the `Runtime::execute_step` actually handle the CodeFix-specific behavior for each step kind? Let me look at what happens when each step kind is executed.

The key question is: when the CodeFix flow runs through the scheduler, does the `PrimitiveExecutor` actually know how to execute `ReadRepository` (Read), `LocateBug` (Compute), `PatchCode` (Write), `RunTests` (Compute), `ValidatePatch` (Route)?

Let me trace through the execution path more carefully.

From the code I've already read:

1. **Contract definition** (`contract.rs:401-407`): `CODEFIX_FLOW` maps each step to a capability
2. **StepKind → PrimitiveKind mapping** (`contract.rs:19-36`):
   - `ReadRepository` → `PrimitiveKind::Read`
   - `LocateBug` → `PrimitiveKind::Compute`
   - `PatchCode` → `PrimitiveKind::Write`
   - `RunTests` → `PrimitiveKind::Compute`
   - `ValidatePatch` → `PrimitiveKind::Route`

3. **ExecSpec generation** (`contract.rs:134-303`): `steps_to_exec_spec` creates step specs with primitives
   - For CodeFix steps, `requires_llm` is true for `PatchCode` and `LocateBug` (line 166-172)
   - `is_analyze` is false for all CodeFix steps (line 173)
   - Since `step.detail` is `None` for CodeFix (from `build_steps` line 118), the `detail_lower` will be empty string
   - So the `else` branch at line 217 is taken for all CodeFix steps
   - This means the payload gets `requires_llm` flag and `operation: "none"`

4. **PrimitiveExecutor** (`primitive_executor.rs`):
   - `Read` (line 19-41): If `path == "repository"`, returns `task_payload`. Otherwise reads from filesystem.
   - `Write` (line 42-79): If `requires_llm` is true, calls LLM to generate patch content, then writes to filesystem.
   - `Compute` (line 80-141): If `operation == "semantic_embedding"`, does embedding. Otherwise if `requires_llm`, calls LLM.
   - `Route` (line 142-167): If `requires_llm`, calls LLM to verify. Otherwise returns "Success".

Now let me think about what's actually implemented vs what's just a contract.

For `ReadRepository` (PrimitiveKind::Read):
- The Read primitive checks if `path == "repository"` and returns the task_payload directly
- But for CodeFix, `detail` is None, so the payload will have `"detail": null`
- The path in the payload comes from the `else` branch which doesn't set a path, so it defaults to "repository" in the Read executor
- So `ReadRepository` will just return the task_payload (the original error text)

For `LocateBug` (PrimitiveKind::Compute, requires_llm=true):
- The Compute primitive with requires_llm=true will call the LLM with a generic prompt
- The prompt is: "You are a deterministic AI kernel worker.\nTask payload:\n{task_payload}\nStep: {step_id}\nExecute this step and return only the result."
- This is generic, not specific to bug location

For `PatchCode` (PrimitiveKind::Write, requires_llm=true):
- The Write primitive with requires_llm=true will call the LLM to generate a patch
- The prompt is: "You are a deterministic AI kernel worker.\nTask payload:\n{task_payload}\nStep: {step_id}\nExecute write patch step and return only the patch/code."
- Then writes to `artifacts/final_patch.txt` (default path)

For `RunTests` (PrimitiveKind::Compute, requires_llm=true):
- Same as LocateBug - calls LLM with generic prompt
- Does NOT actually run `cargo test` or any real test command

For `ValidatePatch` (PrimitiveKind::Route, requires_llm=true):
- Calls LLM with: "You are a deterministic AI kernel verifier.\nTask payload:\n{task_payload}\nStep: {step_id}\nVerify and decide: Success or Failure."
- Returns the LLM's response as `route_decision`

So the key findings are:
1. The contract is fully defined
2. The ExecSpec generation works
3. The primitive executor has implementations for all required kinds
4. BUT the implementations are generic shells, not CodeFix-specific
5. There's no actual test runner, no actual bug detection, no actual code reading from a repository

7: fn payload_for_task(task_id: &str) -> Result<String> {
8:     let path = format!("artifacts/pipeline_input.{}.txt", task_id);
9:     providers::get_filesystem().read_to_string(&path)
10: }
11: 
12: fn default_worker_for_step(step_id: &str) -> &'static str {
13:     let slug = step_id
14:         .split_once('_')
15:         .map(|(_, rest)| rest)
16:         .unwrap_or(step_id);
17: 
18:     match slug {
19:         "analyze_task" | "plan_execution" | "read_repository" | "locate_bug" => "worker-planner",
20:         "execute_changes" | "patch_code" | "run_tests" => "worker-executor",
21:         "validate_patch" | "validate_planner_output" => "worker-verifier",
22:         _ => "worker-planner",
23:     }
24: }
25: 
26: pub fn execute_effects(db: &str, task_id: &str) -> Result<()> {
27:     providers::get_filesystem().create_dir_all("artifacts")?;
28:     let payload = payload_for_task(task_id)?;
29: 
30:     let storage = providers::get_storage();
31:     let spec = storage.load_exec_spec(task_id)?;
32: 
33:     loop {
34:         schedule(db, task_id)?;
35: 
36:         let step_id = if let Some(step_id) = next_ready_step(db, task_id)? {
37:             step_id
38:         } else {
39:             let dispatched = storage.find_dispatched_step(task_id)?;
40:             match dispatched {
41:                 Some(dispatched_step_id) => dispatched_step_id,
42:                 None => break,
43:             }
44:         };
45: 
46:         let worker_id = default_worker_for_step(&step_id).to_string();
47: 
48:         worker::claim_worker(db, task_id, &worker_id)?;
49:         worker::start_step(db, task_id, &worker_id, &step_id)?;
50: 
51:         let mut executed_via_primitive = false;
52:         if let Some(step_spec) = spec.steps.iter().find(|s| s.step_id == step_id) {
53:             if let Some(ref prim) = step_spec.primitive {
54:                 executed_via_primitive = true;
55: 
56:                 match crate::execution::primitive_executor::PrimitiveExecutor::execute(
57:                     task_id, prim, &payload,
58:                 ) {
59:                     Ok(result) => {
60:                         // If it produced a text output (from Compute/Write), save it to final_answer for CLI compatibility
61:                         if let Some(text) = result.output.get("result").and_then(|v| v.as_str()) {
62:                             let answer_path = format!("artifacts/final_answer.{}.txt", task_id);
63:                             providers::get_filesystem().write(&answer_path, text)?;
64:                         }
65: 
66:                         // Generic recording of returned artifacts
67:                         let generation = storage.latest_generation_for_task(task_id)?;
68:                         for artifact in result.artifacts {
69:                             storage.append_semantic_artifact(
70:                                 task_id,
71:                                 &step_id,
72:                                 generation,
73:                                 &artifact.artifact_type,
74:                                 &artifact.payload,
75:                             )?;
76:                         }
77: 
78:                         // Record the raw primitive result as an evidence artifact
79:                         storage.append_semantic_artifact(
80:                             task_id,
81:                             &step_id,
82:                             generation,
83:                             "primitive_result_v1",
84:                             &result.output,
85:                         )?;
86:                     }
87:                     Err(e) => {
88:                         let reason = format!("primitive_execution_error: {e}");
89:                         let _ = worker::fail_step(db, task_id, &worker_id, &step_id, &reason);
90:                         return Err(anyhow!("step {} failed: {}", step_id, reason));
91:                     }
92:                 }
93:             }
94:         }
95: 
96:         if !executed_via_primitive {
97:             return Err(anyhow!(
98:                 "step {} does not have a primitive specification",
99:                 step_id
100:             ));
101:         }
102: 
103:         worker::complete_step(db, task_id, &worker_id, &step_id)?;
104:     }

107:     Ok(())
108: }

1: use crate::workflow::contract::{Step, StepKind};
2: 
3: pub fn normalize_step(raw: &str) -> Option<StepKind> {
4:     let cleaned = raw
5:         .trim()
6:         .trim_start_matches(|c: char| {
7:             c.is_ascii_digit() || c == '.' || c == '-' || c == ')' || c.is_whitespace()
8:         })
9:         .trim()
10:         .trim_matches('`')
11:         .trim()
12:         .trim_end_matches('.');
13: 
14:     if cleaned.is_empty() {
15:         return None;
16:     }
17: 
18:     let lower = cleaned.to_ascii_lowercase();
19:     let tokens: Vec<&str> = lower.split_whitespace().collect();
20: 
21:     let has = |needle: &str| tokens.contains(&needle);
22:     let contains_pair = |a: &str, b: &str| has(a) && has(b);
23: 
24:     if has("deploy")
25:         || has("research")
26:         || has("ux")
27:         || has("readme")
28:         || has("documentation")
29:         || contains_pair("user", "interface")
30:     {
31:         return None;
32:     }
33: 
34:     if contains_pair("parse", "cli") || contains_pair("parse", "arguments") {
35:         return None;
36:     }
37: 
38:     if has("fallback") || has("error") {
39:         return Some(StepKind::AddLlmFallbackHandling);
40:     }
41: 
42:     if has("test") || contains_pair("test", "coverage") || contains_pair("write", "tests") {
43:         return Some(StepKind::AddPlannerTestCoverage);
44:     }
45: 
46:     if contains_pair("validate", "output") || contains_pair("sample", "tasks") {
47:         return Some(StepKind::ValidatePlannerOutput);
48:     }
49: 
50:     if contains_pair("prompt", "shape") || contains_pair("planner", "prompt") {
51:         return Some(StepKind::TightenPlannerPrompt);
52:     }
53: 
54:     if has("normalize") || has("filtering") || has("filter") {
55:         return Some(StepKind::NormalizePlannerOutput);
56:     }
57: 
58:     None
59: }
60: 
61: pub fn parse_steps(text: &str) -> Vec<StepKind> {
62:     let mut out = Vec::new();
63: 
64:     for line in text.lines() {
65:         if let Some(kind) = normalize_step(line) {
66:             if !out.contains(&kind) {
67:                 out.push(kind);
68:             }
69:         }
70:     }
71: 
72:     out
73: }
74: 
75: /// Deterministic preference ordering for planner step kinds.
76: ///
77: /// Contract:
78: /// - `preferred` is only an ordering hint.
79: /// - applying a bias must never add, remove, or duplicate `StepKind`s.
80: /// - different seeds may choose different stable orderings, but the same seed
81: ///   must always produce the same preference vector.
82: #[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq)]
83: // moved to workflow::semantic::bias::SemanticBias
84: pub struct SemanticBias {
85:     pub preferred: Vec<StepKind>,
86: }
87: 
88: /// Maps a replay-safe seed to a deterministic semantic bias.
89: ///
90: /// This function is intentionally small and pure: seed in, preference order out.
91: /// It must remain stable for identical seeds across runs unless the bias contract
92: /// is intentionally versioned and migrated.
93: #[cfg_attr(not(test), allow(dead_code))]
94: pub fn seed_to_bias(seed: u64) -> SemanticBias {
95:     let preferred = if seed.is_multiple_of(2) {
96:         vec![
97:             StepKind::TightenPlannerPrompt,
98:             StepKind::NormalizePlannerOutput,
99:             StepKind::AddLlmFallbackHandling,
100:             StepKind::AddPlannerTestCoverage,
101:             StepKind::ValidatePlannerOutput,
102:         ]
103:     } else {
104:         vec![
105:             StepKind::NormalizePlannerOutput,
106:             StepKind::TightenPlannerPrompt,
107:             StepKind::AddLlmFallbackHandling,
108:             StepKind::AddPlannerTestCoverage,
109:             StepKind::ValidatePlannerOutput,
110:         ]
111:     };
112: 
113:     SemanticBias { preferred }
114: }
115: 
116: /// Applies a stable preference ordering over planner `StepKind`s.
117: ///
118: /// Contract:
119: /// - `None` is a no-op.
120: /// - output is a stable reordering of the input.
121: /// - membership and cardinality are preserved exactly.
122: /// - canonical planner meaning is not enforced here; that is the job of
123: ///   `validate_steps`.
124: pub fn apply_semantic_bias_from_seed(steps: Vec<StepKind>, seed: Option<u64>) -> Vec<StepKind> {
125:     let bias = seed.map(seed_to_bias);
126:     apply_semantic_bias(steps, bias)
127: }
128: 
129: pub fn apply_semantic_bias(steps: Vec<StepKind>, bias: Option<SemanticBias>) -> Vec<StepKind> {
130:     let Some(bias) = bias else {
131:         return steps;
132:     };
133: 
134:     let mut weighted: Vec<(usize, StepKind)> = steps.into_iter().enumerate().collect();
135: 
136:     weighted.sort_by(|a, b| {
137:         let (ia, ka) = a;
138:         let (ib, kb) = b;
139: 
140:         let wa = bias
141:             .preferred
142:             .iter()
143:             .position(|k| k == ka)
144:             .unwrap_or(usize::MAX);
145:         let wb = bias
146:             .preferred
147:             .iter()
148:             .position(|k| k == kb)
149:             .unwrap_or(usize::MAX);
150: 
151:         wa.cmp(&wb).then_with(|| ia.cmp(ib))
152:     });
153: 
154:     weighted.into_iter().map(|(_, kind)| kind).collect()
155: }
156: 
157: /// Projects planner steps onto canonical planner order.
158: ///
159: /// Contract:
160: /// - validation may reorder and deduplicate by canonical workflow order.
161: /// - unlike `apply_semantic_bias`, this function operates on full `Step` values.
162: /// - if no canonical planner step is found, the original input is returned.
163: pub fn validate_steps(steps: Vec<Step>) -> Vec<Step> {
164:     let canonical_order = [
165:         StepKind::TightenPlannerPrompt,
166:         StepKind::NormalizePlannerOutput,
167:         StepKind::AddLlmFallbackHandling,
168:         StepKind::AddPlannerTestCoverage,
169:         StepKind::ValidatePlannerOutput,
170:     ];
171: 
172:     let mut validated = Vec::new();
173: 
174:     for kind in canonical_order {
175:         if let Some(step) = steps.iter().find(|s| s.kind == kind) {
176:             if !validated
177:                 .iter()
178:                 .any(|existing: &Step| existing.kind == step.kind)
179:             {
180:                 validated.push(step.clone());
181:             }
182:         }
183:     }
184: 
185:     if validated.is_empty() {
186:         return steps;
187:     }
188: 
189:     validated
190: }
191:

### 1. Where the Flow Exists in Code

const CODEFIX_FLOW: &[(StepKind, WorkerCapability)] = &[
    (StepKind::ReadRepository, WorkerCapability::Planner),
    (StepKind::LocateBug, WorkerCapability::Planner),
    (StepKind::PatchCode, WorkerCapability::Executor),

fn flow_table(task_class: TaskClass) -> &'static [(StepKind, WorkerCapability)] {
    match task_class {
        TaskClass::Generic => GENERIC_FLOW,
        TaskClass::PlannerHardening => PLANNER_HARDENING_FLOW,

    }
}

### 2. What Is Fully Implemented

**StepKind → PrimitiveKind mapping** — `src/workflow/contract.rs:19-36`:
```
ReadRepository  → PrimitiveKind::Read
LocateBug       → PrimitiveKind::Compute
PatchCode       → PrimitiveKind::Write
RunTests        → PrimitiveKind::Compute
ValidatePatch   → PrimitiveKind::Route
```

**ExecSpec generation** — `src/workflow/contract.rs:134-303`:
- Generates step IDs: `00_read_repository`, `01_locate_bug`, `02_patch_code`, `03_run_tests`, `04_validate_patch`
- `requires_llm = true` for `PatchCode` and `LocateBug` (lines 166-172)
- `is_analyze = false` for all CodeFix steps (line 173)
- Dependency chain: linear (each step depends on the previous)
- Worker constraints: `required_capability` set per step

**Scheduler integration** — `src/effects.rs:12-24`:
```rust
fn default_worker_for_step(step_id: &str) -> &'static str {
    let slug = step_id.split_once('_').map(|(_, rest)| rest).unwrap_or(step_id);
    match slug {
        "analyze_task" | "plan_execution" | "read_repository" | "locate_bug" => "worker-planner",
        "execute_changes" | "patch_code" | "run_tests" => "worker-executor",
        "validate_patch" | "validate_planner_output" => "worker-verifier",
        _ => "worker-planner",
    }
}
```

**Effects execution loop** — `src/effects.rs:26-108`:
- `execute_effects()` drives the full lifecycle: schedule → claim → start → execute primitive → complete
- Calls `PrimitiveExecutor::execute()` for each step (line 56)
- Records `primitive_result_v1` artifacts (line 79-85)
- Processes effect ledger after all steps (line 106)

**Primitive executor implementations** — `src/execution/primitive_executor.rs`:
- `Read` (lines 19-41): Reads from filesystem or returns task_payload
- `Write` (lines 42-79): Writes to filesystem, optionally via LLM
- `Compute` (lines 80-141): Executes computation, optionally via LLM
- `Route` (lines 142-167): Verifies via LLM or returns "Success"

**Test coverage** — `tests/scheduler_integration.rs`:
- `setup_codefix_task()` (lines 131-137): Sets up correct step IDs and dependencies
- `cli_compile_error_routes_to_codefix_flow()` (lines 368-395): Verifies all 5 steps appear
- `codefix_runtime_flow_uses_lease_backed_claim_and_execution()` (lines 411-449): Full lease-backed flow
- `scheduler_treats_all_codefix_sources_the_same()` (lines 398-408): All 3 source types produce identical steps

### 3. What Is Only Contract/Interface

**`TaskInput::detail()` returns `None` for CodeFix** — `src/workflow/compiler.rs:80`:
```rust
TaskInput::CodeFix(_) => None,
```
This means CodeFix steps never receive the artifact reference (the error log path) as `detail` in the ExecSpec. The `artifact_ref` field of `CodeFixInput` is captured but never propagated into step payloads.

**`validate_steps()` ignores CodeFix** — `src/workflow/planner.rs:163-190`:
```rust
pub fn validate_steps(steps: Vec<Step>) -> Vec<Step> {
    let canonical_order = [
        StepKind::TightenPlannerPrompt,
        StepKind::NormalizePlannerOutput,
        StepKind::AddLlmFallbackHandling,
        StepKind::AddPlannerTestCoverage,
        StepKind::ValidatePlannerOutput,
    ];
    // ... only filters against these 5 planner kinds
}
```
CodeFix steps are not in `canonical_order`. If `validate_steps` is called on CodeFix steps, it returns the original input unchanged (line 185-186). This is correct behavior but means CodeFix steps bypass validation entirely.

**`semantic_bias` module is planner-only** — `src/workflow/planner.rs:94-114`:
```rust
pub fn seed_to_bias(seed: u64) -> SemanticBias {
    let preferred = if seed.is_multiple_of(2) {
        vec![
            StepKind::TightenPlannerPrompt,
            StepKind::NormalizePlannerOutput,
            StepKind::AddLlmFallbackHandling,
            StepKind::AddPlannerTestCoverage,
            StepKind::ValidatePlannerOutput,
        ]
    } else { /* ... */ };
    SemanticBias { preferred }
}
```
No CodeFix step kinds appear in the bias preference list.

**Schema validation includes CodeFix kinds** — `src/schema/validator.rs:39-59`:
The `valid_bias()` test fixture includes weights for all 13 step kinds including `ReadRepository`, `LocateBug`, `PatchCode`, `RunTests`, `ValidatePatch`. But the bias system itself doesn't use them.

### 4. Missing Runtime Wiring

**`ReadRepository` does not read a repository** — `src/execution/primitive_executor.rs:19-41`:
```rust
PrimitiveKind::Read => {
    let path = payload.get("path").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("repository");
    let content = if path == "repository" {
        task_payload.to_string()  // Returns the error text, not repo content
    } else {
        providers::get_filesystem().read_to_string(path)?
    };
```
When `path == "repository"` (the default for CodeFix), it returns `task_payload` — the original error text. It does not scan the actual codebase.

**`LocateBug` has no bug detection logic** — `src/execution/primitive_executor.rs:80-141`:
```rust
PrimitiveKind::Compute => {
    // ...
    if requires_llm {
        let prompt = format!(
            "You are a deterministic AI kernel worker.\nTask payload:\n{}\nStep: {}\nExecute this step and return only the result.",
            task_payload, spec.id.0
        );
        let llm_res = providers::get_llm().execute_llm(&prompt, None)?;
```
Generic LLM prompt with no structure. No file parsing, no AST analysis, no diff inspection. The LLM receives the raw error text and must infer the bug location.

**`RunTests` does not run tests** — `src/execution/primitive_executor.rs:80-141`:
Same `Compute` branch as `LocateBug`. No `cargo test`, no test runner invocation, no process execution. The LLM is asked to "execute this step" but has no mechanism to run external commands.

**`PatchCode` writes to a single file** — `src/execution/primitive_executor.rs:42-79`:
```rust
PrimitiveKind::Write => {
    let path = payload.get("path").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("artifacts/final_patch.txt");
    // ...
    providers::get_filesystem().write(path, &content)?;
```
Default path is `artifacts/final_patch.txt`. No multi-file patch application, no git integration, no diff format. The LLM generates raw code and it is written as a single file.

**`ValidatePatch` has no structured verification** — `src/execution/primitive_executor.rs:142-167`:
```rust
PrimitiveKind::Route => {
    let route_decision = if requires_llm {
        let prompt = format!(
            "You are a deterministic AI kernel verifier.\nTask payload:\n{}\nStep: {}\nVerify and decide: Success or Failure.",
            task_payload, spec.id.0
        );
        let llm_res = providers::get_llm().execute_llm(&prompt, None)?;
        llm_res.text  // Raw LLM text, not parsed
    } else {
        "Success".to_string()
    };
```
Returns raw LLM text as `route_decision`. No actual test execution, no compilation check, no diff validation.

**`build_from_task_llm` does not use CodeFix detail** — `src/workflow/compiler.rs:121-159`:
```rust
pub async fn build_from_task_llm(input: &TaskInput) -> Result<Vec<Step>> {
    let normalized = input.detail().unwrap_or("");
    if normalized.is_empty() {
        return Ok(Self::build_steps(input));  // CodeFix always takes this path
    }
```
Since `TaskInput::CodeFix` returns `None` from `detail()`, the LLM refinement path is never triggered for CodeFix. The flow is always the deterministic 5-step sequence.

**No artifact propagation between steps** — `src/workflow/contract.rs:235-246`:
```rust
let inputs = match step.kind {
    StepKind::AnalyzeTask => vec!["task_description".to_string()],
    StepKind::PlanExecution => vec!["analysis_seed".to_string()],
    StepKind::ExecuteChanges => vec!["execution_plan".to_string()],
    _ => vec![],  // All CodeFix steps get empty inputs
};
let outputs = match step.kind {
    StepKind::AnalyzeTask => vec!["analysis_seed".to_string()],
    StepKind::PlanExecution => vec!["execution_plan".to_string()],
    StepKind::ExecuteChanges => vec!["changes_committed".to_string()],
    _ => vec![],  // All CodeFix steps get empty outputs
};
```
CodeFix steps declare no inputs or outputs. The output of `ReadRepository` is not wired as input to `LocateBug`. Each step operates independently.

**No error log parsing** — The `CodeFixInput.artifact_ref` field (the path to the error log) is stored but never read by any runtime code. The `effects.rs` loop reads task payload from `artifacts/pipeline_input.{task_id}.txt` (line 8), which is populated by `pipeline-run`, not by the CodeFix flow.

## Assistant (Build · MiMo Auto · 12.8s)

_Thinking:_

Now I have a complete picture. Let me compile the verification results.

The 5 CodeFix steps map to 4 distinct PrimitiveKind variants:
1. ReadRepository → PrimitiveKind::Read
2. LocateBug → PrimitiveKind::Compute
3. PatchCode → PrimitiveKind::Write
4. RunTests → PrimitiveKind::Compute (same as LocateBug)
5. ValidatePatch → PrimitiveKind::Route

So there are 4 unique primitive kinds: Read, Write, Compute, Route.

Let me trace each one:

**PrimitiveKind::Read** - `src/execution/primitive_executor.rs:19-41`
- Implementation: Reads from filesystem or returns task_payload
- Execution path: `Runtime::execute_step` → `PrimitiveExecutor::execute` → `PrimitiveKind::Read` branch
- Test: `test_file_write_read_and_command_primitives` in `src/planner_pipeline/execution_engine.rs:826-903` (tests "Step 1 read file" which triggers Read path)
- Also tested: `step_kind_to_primitive_kind_mapping` in `src/execution_abi/primitives/mod.rs:67-84` (verifies ReadRepository → Read mapping)

**PrimitiveKind::Write** - `src/execution/primitive_executor.rs:42-79`
- Implementation: Writes to filesystem, optionally via LLM
- Execution path: `Runtime::execute_step` → `PrimitiveExecutor::execute` → `PrimitiveKind::Write` branch
- Test: `test_file_write_read_and_command_primitives` in `src/planner_pipeline/execution_engine.rs:826-903` (tests "Step 1 write file" which triggers Write path)
- Also tested: `step_kind_to_primitive_kind_mapping` in `src/execution_abi/primitives/mod.rs:67-84` (verifies PatchCode → Write mapping)

**PrimitiveKind::Compute** - `src/execution/primitive_executor.rs:80-141`
- Implementation: Executes computation, optionally via LLM; also handles semantic_embedding
- Execution path: `Runtime::execute_step` → `PrimitiveExecutor::execute` → `PrimitiveKind::Compute` branch
- Test: `test_file_write_read_and_command_primitives` in `src/planner_pipeline/execution_engine.rs:826-903` (tests "Step 1 run command echo" which triggers Compute path)
- Also tested: `step_kind_to_primitive_kind_mapping` in `src/execution_abi/primitives/mod.rs:67-84` (verifies AnalyzeTask → Compute mapping)
- Also tested: `primitives_roundtrip_json` in `src/execution_abi/primitives/mod.rs:52-64` (tests Compute serialization)

**PrimitiveKind::Route** - `src/execution/primitive_executor.rs:142-167`
- Implementation: Verifies via LLM or returns "Success"
- Execution path: `Runtime::execute_step` → `PrimitiveExecutor::execute` → `PrimitiveKind::Route` branch
- Test: `step_kind_to_primitive_kind_mapping` in `src/execution_abi/primitives/mod.rs:67-84` (verifies ValidatePatch → Route mapping)

## PrimitiveKind Definitions

**File**: `src/execution_abi/primitives/mod.rs:6-17`
```rust
pub enum PrimitiveKind {
    Compute,
    Read,
    Write,
    Route,
    Wait,
    Signal,
    Spawn,
    Complete,
    Fail,
}
```

9 variants declared. The CodeFix flow uses 4 of them (LocateBug and RunTests both map to Compute).

---

## Verification by PrimitiveKind

### PrimitiveKind::Read

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Declaration** | `src/execution_abi/primitives/mod.rs:9` |
| **Implementation** | `src/execution/primitive_executor.rs:19-41` |
| **StepKind mapping** | `ReadRepository → Read` (`src/workflow/contract.rs:30`) |
| **Execution path** | Reads `payload.path`; if `"repository"` returns `task_payload`, else calls `providers::get_filesystem().read_to_string(path)` |
| **Mapping test** | `step_kind_to_primitive_kind_mapping` at `src/execution_abi/primitives/mod.rs:67-71` |
| **Execution test** | `test_file_write_read_and_command_primitives` at `src/planner_pipeline/execution_engine.rs:852-855` — tests `"Step 1 read file {path}"` which triggers the Read branch via `execute_primitive()` |

**Verdict**: Executable. Tested for mapping and execution.

---

### PrimitiveKind::Write

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Declaration** | `src/execution_abi/primitives/mod.rs:10` |
| **Implementation** | `src/execution/primitive_executor.rs:42-79` |
| **StepKind mapping** | `PatchCode → Write` (`src/workflow/contract.rs:32`) |
| **Execution path** | Reads `payload.path` (default `"artifacts/final_patch.txt"`); if `requires_llm` calls LLM for content, else reads `payload.content`; writes via `providers::get_filesystem().write(path, &content)` |
| **Mapping test** | `step_kind_to_primitive_kind_mapping` at `src/execution_abi/primitives/mod.rs:72-75` |
| **Execution test** | `test_file_write_read_and_command_primitives` at `src/planner_pipeline/execution_engine.rs:837-850` — tests `"Step 1 write file {path} with hello_world_content"` which triggers the Write branch; verifies `std::fs::read_to_string(path) == "hello_world_content"` |

**Verdict**: Executable. Tested for mapping and execution.

---

### PrimitiveKind::Compute

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Declaration** | `src/execution_abi/primitives/mod.rs:8` |
| **Implementation** | `src/execution/primitive_executor.rs:80-141` |
| **StepKind mapping** | `LocateBug → Compute` (`src/workflow/contract.rs:31`), `RunTests → Compute` (`src/workflow/contract.rs:33`) |
| **Execution path** | Two branches: (1) `operation == "semantic_embedding"` → calls `providers::get_llm().embed_text(detail)`, produces `analysis_seed` artifact; (2) else if `requires_llm` → calls `providers::get_llm().execute_llm(prompt)`; else returns `{"status": "computed", "task_id": ...}` |
| **Mapping test** | `step_kind_to_primitive_kind_mapping` at `src/execution_abi/primitives/mod.rs:76-79` (tests `AnalyzeTask → Compute`) |
| **Execution test** | `test_file_write_read_and_command_primitives` at `src/planner_pipeline/execution_engine.rs:857-860` — tests `"Step 1 run command echo test_command_success"` which triggers the Compute branch via `execute_primitive()` in `execution_engine.rs:296-331` (runs `std::process::Command::new("echo").arg("test_command_success")`) |
| **Serialization test** | `primitives_roundtrip_json` at `src/execution_abi/primitives/mod.rs:52-64` — tests Compute roundtrip |

**Verdict**: Executable. Tested for mapping, execution, and serialization. Two distinct sub-paths (embedding vs command).

---

### PrimitiveKind::Route

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Declaration** | `src/execution_abi/primitives/mod.rs:11` |
| **Implementation** | `src/execution/primitive_executor.rs:142-167` |
| **StepKind mapping** | `ValidatePatch → Route` (`src/workflow/contract.rs:34`) |
| **Execution path** | If `requires_llm` → calls `providers::get_llm().execute_llm(prompt)`, returns raw LLM text as `route_decision`; else returns `"Success"` |
| **Mapping test** | `step_kind_to_primitive_kind_mapping` at `src/execution_abi/primitives/mod.rs:80-83` |
| **Execution test** | **NONE** |

No test in the entire codebase exercises `PrimitiveExecutor::execute` with `PrimitiveKind::Route`. The only test for Route is the static mapping assertion. The `execute_primitive` function in `execution_engine.rs:333-341` handles Route via the `_ =>` catch-all branch, but no test invokes it with a Route primitive.

**Verdict**: **Declared and implemented, but NOT execution-tested**.

## Summary

| PrimitiveKind | StepKind(s) | Implementation | Mapping Test | Execution Test |
|---------------|-------------|----------------|--------------|----------------|
| `Read` | `ReadRepository` | `primitive_executor.rs:19-41` | `primitives/mod.rs:67-71` | `execution_engine.rs:852-855` |
| `Write` | `PatchCode` | `primitive_executor.rs:42-79` | `primitives/mod.rs:72-75` | `execution_engine.rs:837-850` |
| `Compute` | `LocateBug`, `RunTests` | `primitive_executor.rs:80-141` | `primitives/mod.rs:76-79` | `execution_engine.rs:857-860` |
| **`Route`** | `ValidatePatch` | `primitive_executor.rs:142-167` | `primitives/mod.rs:80-83` | **NONE** |

**Correction to my previous statement**: "all 5 primitive kinds are implemented" is accurate for code existence — all 4 unique `PrimitiveKind` variants have match arms in `PrimitiveExecutor::execute`. However, `PrimitiveKind::Route` has **no execution test**. It is only verified at the static mapping level (`StepKind::ValidatePatch.to_primitive_kind() == PrimitiveKind::Route`). The LLM-dependent path and the default `"Success"` path in the Route branch are both untested.

10:     println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(report)?);
11:     Ok(())
12: }

14: #[allow(dead_code)]
15: pub fn emit_json(command: &str, report: Value) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
16:     let envelope = command_report(command, report);
17:     println!("{}", serde_json::to_string(&envelope)?);
18:     Ok(())
19: }

21: #[allow(dead_code)]
22: pub fn command_report(command: &str, report: Value) -> Value {
23:     serde_json::json!({
24:         "ok": true,
25:         "schema_version": cli_json_schema_version(),
26:         "command": command,
27:         "report": report,

52: pub struct ComparisonReportInput<'a> {
53:     pub left_task_id: &'a str,
54:     pub right_task_id: &'a str,
55:     pub left_capsule: &'a ReplayCapsule,
56:     pub right_capsule: &'a ReplayCapsule,
57:     pub left_valid: bool,
58:     pub right_valid: bool,
59:     pub status: &'a str,
60:     pub explanation: &'a str,
61:     pub left_only_events: Vec<String>,
62:     pub right_only_events: Vec<String>,
63:     pub left_only_nodes: Vec<Value>,
64:     pub right_only_nodes: Vec<Value>,
65:     pub left_only_edges: Vec<Value>,
66:     pub right_only_edges: Vec<Value>,
67: }
68: 
69: pub fn comparison_report(input: ComparisonReportInput<'_>) -> Value {
70:     serde_json::json!({
71:         "ok": true,
72:         "schema_version": cli_json_schema_version(),
73:         "left": {
74:             "task_id": input.left_task_id,
75:             "capsule_id": input.left_capsule.capsule_id,
76:             "valid": input.left_valid,
77:             "events": input.left_capsule.event_ids.len(),
78:             "nodes": input.left_capsule.state_graph.nodes.len(),
79:             "edges": input.left_capsule.state_graph.edges.len(),
80:         },
81:         "right": {
82:             "task_id": input.right_task_id,
83:             "capsule_id": input.right_capsule.capsule_id,
84:             "valid": input.right_valid,
85:             "events": input.right_capsule.event_ids.len(),
86:             "nodes": input.right_capsule.state_graph.nodes.len(),
87:             "edges": input.right_capsule.state_graph.edges.len(),
88:         },
89:         "status": input.status,
90:         "explanation": input.explanation,
91:         "diff": {
92:             "event_ids": {
93:                 "left_only": input.left_only_events,
94:                 "right_only": input.right_only_events,
95:             },
96:             "nodes": {
97:                 "left_only": input.left_only_nodes,
98:                 "right_only": input.right_only_nodes,
99:             },
100:             "edges": {
101:                 "left_only": input.left_only_edges,
102:                 "right_only": input.right_only_edges,
103:             }
104:         }
105:     })
106: }

48:     fn classify_failure_outcome(reason: &str) -> StepOutcome {
49:         let lower = reason.trim().to_ascii_lowercase();
50: 
51:         if lower.starts_with("retry:")
52:             || lower.starts_with("transient:")
53:             || lower.starts_with("timeout")
54:         {
55:             return StepOutcome::RetryableFailure;
56:         }

62:             return StepOutcome::Blocked;
63:         }
64:

66:     }

70:             StepOutcome::Success => "STEP_COMPLETED",
71:             StepOutcome::RetryableFailure | StepOutcome::TerminalFailure | StepOutcome::Blocked => {
72:                 "STEP_FAILED"

74:         }

## Assistant (Build · MiMo Auto · 42.8s)

Now I have enough evidence to compile the production readiness audit. Let me organize all the findings.

Now I have all the evidence. Here is the audit.

---

# Production Readiness Audit

## Finding 1 — `unsafe set_var` in production binary

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | HIGH |
| **File** | `src/main.rs:17-19` |
| **Symbol** | `suppress_nested_cargo_warnings()` |
| **Evidence** | `unsafe { std::env::set_var("RUSTFLAGS", "-Awarnings"); }` — `set_var` is undefined behavior in multi-threaded programs since Rust 1.66. Called unconditionally at the start of `main()` before `#[tokio::main]` spawns threads. |
| **Direction** | Remove the unsafe call. Use `RUSTFLAGS` at build time or pass `-Awarnings` via `.cargo/config.toml`. |

---

## Finding 2 — Connection opened + schema re-executed on every call

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | HIGH |
| **File** | `src/providers/storage.rs:154-166` |
| **Symbol** | `DefaultStorage::conn()` |
| **Evidence** | Every `StorageProvider` method calls `self.conn()`, which opens a new `rusqlite::Connection` and runs `conn.execute_batch(include_str!("../../event_bus/schema.sql"))` (141 lines of DDL) on every single call. No connection pooling, no cached connection. |
| **Direction** | Pool or cache the connection (e.g., `OnceLock<Connection>` with WAL mode, or a connection pool). Remove the per-call schema execution — run DDL once at startup. |

---

## Finding 3 — `set_override_path` thread-local is not RAII-guarded in production code

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | HIGH |
| **File** | `src/scheduler.rs:4-45`, `src/worker.rs:3-42`, `src/leases.rs:3-15` |
| **Symbol** | All 6 scheduler functions, all 5 worker functions, both lease functions |
| **Evidence** | Every function follows the pattern: `set_override_path(Some(db)); let res = ...; set_override_path(None); res`. If the inner call panics, the override is never cleared. 17 instances across 3 files. The `TestDbGuard` RAII pattern exists in tests but is not used in production code. |
| **Direction** | Introduce a production RAII guard (e.g., `OverrideGuard` with `Drop`) or restructure the API to pass the DB path directly rather than using thread-local state. |

---

## Finding 4 — `reqwest::Client` created per LLM request

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | MEDIUM |
| **File** | `src/llm.rs:78` |
| **Symbol** | `chat_with_model_override()` |
| **Evidence** | `let client = reqwest::Client::new();` inside an async function called for every LLM invocation. `reqwest::Client` internally manages a connection pool and TLS session cache — recreating it defeats this. |
| **Direction** | Create the client once (e.g., `OnceLock<reqwest::Client>` or store in the provider struct) and reuse across requests. |

---

## Finding 5 — No timeout on LLM HTTP requests

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | MEDIUM |
| **File** | `src/llm.rs:78-84` |
| **Symbol** | `chat_with_model_override()` |
| **Evidence** | The `reqwest::Client` is created with default config (no timeout). The `.send().await?` call can hang indefinitely if the LLM server is unresponsive. |
| **Direction** | Set a connection and read timeout on the client (e.g., `Client::builder().timeout(Duration::from_secs(120))`). |

---

## Finding 6 — `classify_failure_outcome` duplicated with divergent logic

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | MEDIUM |
| **File** | `src/providers/storage.rs:392-400` vs `src/worker.rs:48-66` (test-only copy) |
| **Symbol** | `classify_failure_outcome` |
| **Evidence** | Production version (`storage.rs`): matches `"retry:"` or `"timeout waiting for lock"`. Test copy (`worker.rs`): matches `"retry:"`, `"transient:"`, `"timeout"`, `"blocked:"`, `"waiting_on:"`, `"dependency:"`. The test copy is more permissive and does not match the production logic. The test copy is not `#[cfg(test)]` on the function itself — it is inside `mod tests` but could diverge further. |
| **Direction** | Consolidate into a single function in a shared module. The test should test the production function, not a duplicate. |

---

## Finding 7 — `outcome_to_event_type` duplicated

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | LOW |
| **File** | `src/providers/storage.rs:402-408` vs `src/worker.rs:68-75` |
| **Symbol** | `outcome_to_event_type` |
| **Evidence** | Two independent implementations with identical logic. |
| **Direction** | Extract to a shared module (same as Finding 6). |

---

## Finding 8 — LLM client not injectable via provider trait

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | MEDIUM |
| **File** | `src/providers/mod.rs:91-93`, `src/llm.rs:78` |
| **Symbol** | `get_llm()`, `chat_with_model_override()` |
| **Evidence** | `get_llm()` returns a `&'static dyn LlmProvider`, but `chat_with_model_override()` in `llm.rs` directly creates `reqwest::Client::new()` and calls the OpenAI API — it does NOT go through the `LlmProvider` trait. The trait has `execute_llm()` and `embed_text()`, but the `llm.rs` module bypasses the trait entirely. |
| **Direction** | Route all LLM calls through the `LlmProvider` trait. The `llm.rs` functions should delegate to `get_llm()` rather than making direct HTTP calls. |

---

## Finding 9 — `TaskInput::CodeFix` detail never propagated to ExecSpec

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | HIGH |
| **File** | `src/workflow/compiler.rs:80` |
| **Symbol** | `TaskInput::detail()` |
| **Evidence** | `TaskInput::CodeFix(_) => None` — the `artifact_ref` (path to error log) is captured in `CodeFixInput` but `detail()` returns `None`. `build_steps()` (line 118) calls `step_specs_to_steps(&specs, None)`, so all CodeFix steps get `detail: None`. The error log path is never injected into any step payload. |
| **Direction** | Propagate `CodeFixInput.artifact_ref` as `detail` into the ExecSpec steps, or inject it into the task payload that `effects.rs` reads. |

---

## Finding 10 — `validate_steps` does not validate CodeFix steps

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | LOW |
| **File** | `src/workflow/planner.rs:163-190` |
| **Symbol** | `validate_steps()` |
| **Evidence** | The canonical order only contains the 5 PlannerHardening step kinds. If CodeFix steps are passed in, the function returns them unchanged (line 185-186 `if validated.is_empty() { return steps; }`). This is technically correct but means CodeFix steps bypass validation entirely. |
| **Direction** | Either extend `validate_steps` to handle CodeFix steps or document that CodeFix steps are intentionally unvalidated. |

---

## Finding 11 — Replay capsule `created_at` is hardcoded to `"now"`

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | LOW |
| **File** | `src/replay/capsule.rs:17` |
| **Symbol** | `build_replay_capsule()` |
| **Evidence** | `created_at: "now".to_string()` — not a timestamp, not ISO 8601, just the string `"now"`. This breaks any downstream code that tries to parse or compare timestamps. |
| **Direction** | Use `chrono::Utc::now().to_rfc3339()` or similar. |

---

## Finding 12 — `replay_capsule.rs` hardcodes `artifacts: vec![]` and `decision_points: vec![]`

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | MEDIUM |
| **File** | `src/replay/capsule.rs:20-21` |
| **Symbol** | `build_replay_capsule()` |
| **Evidence** | The capsule builder always produces empty `artifacts` and `decision_points` vectors, even though `semantic_artifacts` table contains artifacts and the `ReplayCapsule` struct supports both fields. The `determinism_envelope` is also static JSON, not derived from actual execution data. |
| **Direction** | Populate `artifacts` from `semantic_artifacts` table. Populate `decision_points` from event log where applicable. |

---

## Finding 13 — `DefaultLlm` does not implement async via trait correctly

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | MEDIUM |
| **File** | `src/providers/mod.rs:38-79` |
| **Symbol** | `DefaultLlm` |
| **Evidence** | `DefaultLlm::execute_llm()` and `embed_text()` use `tokio::task::block_in_place` + `Handle::current().block_on()` to bridge sync→async. This requires a tokio multi-thread runtime to be active. If called from a non-tokio context or from a tokio runtime with `current_thread` flavor, it will panic. |
| **Direction** | Make the `LlmProvider` trait async (`async fn`), or document the tokio runtime requirement. |

---

## Finding 14 — `state_hash` is payload length, not a real hash

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | LOW |
| **File** | `src/providers/storage.rs:1020` |
| **Symbol** | `rebuild_snapshot()` |
| **Evidence** | `let state_hash = serde_json::to_string(&state_payload)?.len() as u64;` — the "hash" is the byte length of the JSON string, not a cryptographic hash. Two different payloads with the same serialized length would produce the same "hash". |
| **Direction** | Use `blake3::hash(...)` like the rest of the codebase. |

---

## Finding 15 — 41 `unwrap()` calls in `main.rs` with no error context

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | MEDIUM |
| **File** | `src/main.rs:24-1199` (41 instances) |
| **Symbol** | Various CLI command handlers |
| **Evidence** | `Connection::open(db).unwrap()`, `serde_json::from_str(&payload).unwrap()`, `std::fs::create_dir_all("artifacts").unwrap()`, etc. Any of these will produce a panic backtrace with no user-facing error message. |
| **Direction** | Replace with `.unwrap_or_else(|e| { eprintln!("...: {e}"); std::process::exit(1); })` or use the `?` pattern with a top-level error handler. |

---

## Finding 16 — `#[allow(dead_code)]` on 33 items across the codebase

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | LOW |
| **File** | Multiple files (see grep results) |
| **Symbol** | `cli_json::print_json_report`, `cli_json::emit_json`, `cli_json::command_report`, `cli_json::capsule_summary_report`, `event_bus::append_event`, `event_bus::commit_causal_unit`, `event_bus::query_events`, `event_bus::publish_pipeline_report`, `event_bus::publish_pipeline_failed`, `scheduler::unlock_ready_steps_by_db`, `kernel_types::ReplayCapsule::is_minimally_valid`, `llm::role_for_purpose`, `llm::chat`, `lm_control::ModelsResponse`, `lm_control::ModelInfo`, `workflow::compiler::Workflow::from_plan_steps`, `workflow::contract::CONTRACT_VERSION`, `workflow::contract::contract_version`, `workflow::contract::task_class_names`, `workflow::contract::step_kind_names`, `workflow::contract::outcome_names`, `workflow::contract::event_type_names`, `workflow::contract::advisory_capability_for_step`, `schema::validator` (entire module) |
| **Evidence** | 33 `#[allow(dead_code)]` annotations. Many of these are public API functions that are only called from tests or CLI, not from library code. |
| **Direction** | Audit each: if the function is part of the public API, remove the annotation. If it is truly unused, delete it. |

---

## Finding 17 — `register_*` provider functions silently fail

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | LOW |
| **File** | `src/providers/mod.rs:101-111` |
| **Symbol** | `register_filesystem()`, `register_llm()`, `register_storage()` |
| **Evidence** | All three use `let _ = PROVIDER.set(provider);` — the `OnceLock::set` return value (which indicates whether the value was already set) is silently discarded. If called twice, the second call is silently ignored with no error. |
| **Direction** | Return `Result` or `bool` from the registration functions, or panic in debug mode if already set. |

---

## Finding 18 — `OVERRIDE_PATH` has no cleanup on panic

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | MEDIUM |
| **File** | `src/providers/storage.rs:134-136` |
| **Symbol** | `OVERRIDE_PATH` thread-local |
| **Evidence** | `thread_local! { static OVERRIDE_PATH: RefCell<Option<String>> = ... }` — if a function panics between `set_override_path(Some(...))` and `set_override_path(None)`, the override persists for the thread. In a long-running server or test harness, this causes subsequent operations to use the wrong database. |
| **Direction** | Use a RAII guard (`struct OverrideGuard` with `Drop` that clears the path). |

---

## Finding 19 — No concurrency protection on `OVERRIDE_PATH`

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | LOW |
| **File** | `src/providers/storage.rs:134-136` |
| **Symbol** | `OVERRIDE_PATH` |
| **Evidence** | Thread-local storage means each thread has its own override. But `effects.rs:26-108` calls `set_override_path` and then performs multiple operations in a loop — if another thread on the same tokio worker sets a different override, the thread-local is safe, but the design assumes single-threaded execution per task. |
| **Direction** | Document the thread-local assumption. Consider passing DB path as a parameter instead. |

---

## Finding 20 — `effects.rs` reads task payload from filesystem, not from event log

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **Severity** | MEDIUM |
| **File** | `src/effects.rs:7-10` |
| **Symbol** | `payload_for_task()` |
| **Evidence** | `fn payload_for_task(task_id: &str) -> Result<String> { let path = format!("artifacts/pipeline_input.{}.txt", task_id); providers::get_filesystem().read_to_string(&path) }` — The CodeFix flow never writes `artifacts/pipeline_input.{task_id}.txt`. This file is only created by `pipeline-run` in `main.rs:920-928`. If `execute_effects` is called for a CodeFix task that was not created via `pipeline-run`, it will fail with a file-not-found error. |
| **Direction** | Either ensure all task creation paths write the input file, or read the task payload from the `tasks` table or event log instead. |

---

## Summary by Severity
